If every component of
L
is of finite type, then

V_{L}=\operatorname{span}(L)\oplus L^{\perp}
where
\operatorname{span}(L)
and
L^{\perp}
are
B
-orthogonal.

(d)

If any one of the conditions
1.53
(i)–(iii) holds for some object
J
of a connected Brink-Howlett groupoid
G
, then it holds for every object of
G
.

Proof.

Parts (a)–(b) are trivial.

We prove (c). Clearly,
\operatorname{span}(L)
and
L^{\perp}
are
B
-orthogonal for any subset
L
of
\Pi
. Assume every component of
L
is of finite type. Then in particular,
B
is positive definite on
\operatorname{span}(L)
, which implies that
the sum
\operatorname{span}(L)+L^{\perp}
is direct, since for
v\in\operatorname{span}(L)\cap L^{\perp}
, we have

0\leq B(v,v)\in B(\operatorname{span}(L),L^{\perp})\subseteq\{0\}

and hence
v=0
. Clearly,
L^{\perp}\subseteq V_{L}
, and we have
\operatorname{span}(L)\subseteq V_{L}
since every component of
L
is finite. Hence
\operatorname{span}(L)+L^{\perp}\subseteq V_{L}
.

To complete the proof of (c), it remains to show that if
v\in V_{L}
, then
v\in\operatorname{span}(L)+L^{\perp}
. Let
J
be the union of all components of
L
which contain a root which is not orthogonal to
v
.
By the assumptions,
J
is a finite union of components of
L
, so
\operatorname{span}(J)\perp\operatorname{span}(L\setminus J)
. We also have
v\perp\operatorname{span}(L\setminus J)
by the definition of
J
.
Since all components of
L
are of finite type finite,
J
is a spherical subset of
\Pi
. Since
B
is positive definite on
\operatorname{span}(J)
, we may choose a basis

v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}
of
\operatorname{span}(J)
such that
B(v_{i},v_{j})=\delta_{i,j}
(the Kronecker delta). Let
v^{\prime}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}B(v,v_{i})v_{i}\in\operatorname{span}(J)\subseteq\operatorname{span}(L)
and
v^{\prime\prime}:=v-v^{\prime}
. We have
B(v,v_{i})=B(v^{\prime},v_{i})
for
i=1,\ldots,n
, so
v^{\prime\prime}=v-v^{\prime}\in J^{\perp}
. Also,
v,v^{\prime}\in(L\setminus J)^{\perp}
so
v^{\prime\prime}\in(L\setminus J)^{\perp}
. Hence
v^{\prime\prime}\in L^{\perp}
,
v^{\prime}\in\operatorname{span}(L)
and
v=v^{\prime}+v^{\prime\prime}
, as required.

To prove (d), let
(K,w,J)
be a morphism of a Brink-Howlett groupoid
G
. We are to show that if
J
satisfies any one of the conditions
1.53
(i)–(iii), then that same condition holds with
J
replaced by
K
. For (i) and (ii), this follows since, by Proposition
4.7
(a), the symmetric difference
J_{\mathrm{fin}}+K_{\mathrm{fin}}
is finite, and
J_{\inf}=K_{\inf}
. For (iii), it suffices by (c) to show that
V_{J_{\mathrm{fin}}}=V_{K_{\mathrm{fin}}}
; this holds since the definitions imply that
V_{L}=V_{L^{\prime}}
if
L,L^{\prime}\subseteq\Pi
with
L+L^{\prime}
finite.
∎

6.3.

As shown in
[
9
]
, the study of connected Brink-Howlett
groupoids
G
in general can be reduced to the case when each object
J
of
G

satisfies the conditions
1.53
(i)–(ii). Propositions
4.7
–
4.9
show that the study of such a groupoid
G
and its (abstract) real roots can be reduced to the situation when each object
J
of
G

satisfies
1.53
(i)–(iii); Theorem
1.49
(d) then implies that the corresponding systems of real roots can be faithfully realized in real vector spaces. These conditions lead to useful extra structure, such as an analogue of the
W
-invariant bilinear form
B
on
V
, and some gains in simplicity of statements, with little loss for many purposes, and we impose them henceforward.

Assumption 6.4
.

For the remainder of this paper, we assume unless otherwise stated that each object
J
of
G
satisfies conditions
1.53
(i)–(iii). We consider the weakly realized
signed groupoid set
(G,\Upsilon_{X},{\mathcal{V}},\iota)
where
X
denotes either
P
,
M
or
R
, and adopt the associated notation and identifications described in
1.55
–
1.54
. In particular, for an object
J
of
J
,
\pi_{J}
denotes the orthogonal projection
V\to\mathcal{V}(J)=J^{\perp}
.

6.5.

Let
F
be a subset of
\Pi
such that
F=F_{\mathrm{fin}}
and
V=\operatorname{span}(F)+F^{\perp}
.
Let
\pi_{F}\colon V\to F^{\perp}
denote the orthogonal projection determined by this orthogonal direct sum. This extends the notation already introduced in case
F\in\operatorname{ob}(G)
.

The projection
\pi_{F}
may be alternatively described as follows. Given
\beta\in V
, choose a finite subset
M
of
F

such that
M
is a union of components of
F
and
\{\,\alpha\in F\mid B(\alpha,\beta)\neq 0\,\}\subseteq M
. Then
M
is a spherical subset of
\Pi
, and

(6.5.1)

\pi_{F}(\beta)=\frac{1}{|W_{M}|}\sum_{w\in W_{M}}w\beta.

We leave the verification of this formula to the reader.
Note that it implies
\pi_{F}(\beta)=\pi_{M}(\beta)
, as can easily be checked by other means (e.g. using Proposition
6.7
(a) below).

We may sometimes write
F^{\perp}
as
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{F}}}}V^{{\kern-6.05559pt\kern 6.44447pt}}_{{\kern-10.38712pt{F}\kern 6.44447pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{F}}}}V^{{\kern-6.05559pt\kern 6.44447pt}}_{{\kern-10.38712pt{F}\kern 6.44447pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{F}}}}V^{{\kern-3.55832pt\kern 3.9472pt}}_{{\kern-6.79512pt{F}\kern 3.9472pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{F}}}}V^{{\kern-2.43054pt\kern 2.81943pt}}_{{\kern-5.66734pt{F}\kern 2.81943pt}}}
or,
if
F\in\operatorname{ob}(G)
, as
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{F}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.84546pt{F}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{F}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.84546pt{F}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{F}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.52292pt{F}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{F}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.47292pt{F}\kern 2.625pt}}}
.
For notational compactness, we shall often write
v_{F}:=\pi_{F}(v)
for
v\in V
.

Subsets
F\subseteq\Pi
such that
F=F_{\mathrm{fin}}
and
V=\operatorname{span}(F)\oplus F^{\perp}
include any
F\subseteq\Pi
such that
W_{F}
is a finite index overgroup of
W_{J}
where
J
is an object of
G
, and any spherical subset
F
of
\Pi
.
In particular, they include the sets
K
and
L=J\cup K
where
J\in\operatorname{ob}(G)
and
K\subseteq\Pi
is such that
\nu(K,J)
is defined.

We shall require below some properties of the projection
\pi_{L}
, for suitable
L\subseteq\Pi
, which we prove by checking the existence of a suitable analogue of “fundamental weights” for
L
in
\operatorname{span}(L)
. The proof reduces to the case where
L
is finite and irreducible, in which case the relevant properties are known from Vinberg’s lemma (
[
32
, Ch 4]
), Perron-Frobenius theory
(see for example
[
36
]
) or
[
5
, Ch 5, §3, no. 5]
.
If
\beta\in L
, we write
L_{\beta}
for the component of
L
containing
\beta
.

Proposition 6.6
.

Suppose that
L\subseteq\Pi
has only finite-type components.

(a)

There is a unique basis
(\omega^{L}_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in L}
of
\operatorname{span}(L)
such that for all
\alpha,\beta\in L
, one has
B(\omega^{L}_{\alpha},\beta)=\delta_{\alpha,\beta}
(where
\delta_{\alpha,\beta}\in\mathbb{R}
is the Kronecker delta).

(b)

Let
\beta\in L
. Write
\omega_{\beta}^{L}=\sum_{\alpha\in L}a^{L}_{\alpha,\beta}\alpha
with each
a_{\alpha,\beta}^{L}\in\mathbb{R}
. Then
a^{L}_{\alpha,\beta}\neq 0\iff\alpha\in L_{\beta}\iff a^{L}_{\alpha,\beta}>0
.

(c)

If
\alpha,\beta\in L
, then
B(\omega_{\alpha}^{L},\omega_{\beta}^{L})=a^{L}_{\alpha,\beta}
. Hence
B(\omega_{\alpha}^{L},\omega_{\beta}^{L})>0
if
L_{\alpha}=L_{\beta}
and

B(\omega_{\alpha}^{L},\omega_{\beta}^{L})=0
otherwise.

(d)

For any
\beta\in L
,
\omega_{\beta}^{L}
is an element of
\operatorname{cone}(L_{\beta})
.

Remark
.

(1) We often abbreviate
\omega^{L}_{\alpha}=\omega_{\alpha}
and
a^{L}_{\alpha,\beta}=a_{\alpha,\beta}
if
L
is understood.

(2) In (d),
\omega_{\beta}^{L}
is, more precisely, in the relative interior of
\operatorname{cone}(L_{\beta})
.

Proof.

We first prove (a)–(b) in the special case in which
L
is irreducible and hence finite. By
[
5
, Ch 5, §3, no. 5, Lemme 3]
,
L
is linearly independent. Part (a) is then clear since the restriction of
B
to
\operatorname{span}(L)
is positive definite and hence non-singular.

For
\beta\in L
, write
\omega_{\beta}=\sum_{\alpha\in L}a_{\alpha,\beta}^{L}\alpha
where each
a_{\alpha,\beta}^{L}\in\mathbb{R}
. We have to show that
a^{L}_{\alpha,\beta}>0
for all
\alpha,\beta\in L
. Fix
\beta\in L
. Since
B(\omega^{L}_{\beta},\alpha)\geq 0
for all
\alpha\in L
,
[
5
, Ch 5, §3, no. 5, Lemme 6(ii)]
shows that
a^{L}_{\alpha,\beta}\geq 0
for all
\alpha\in L
. Suppose
that
a^{L}_{\alpha,\beta}=0
for some
\alpha\in L
. Since
L\neq{\emptyset}
, one has
\omega^{L}_{\beta}\neq 0
and so
a_{\alpha^{\prime},\beta}\neq 0
for some
\alpha^{\prime}\in L
. Since
L
is connected, there must exist
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in L
such that
\alpha
is joined (by an edge) to
\alpha^{\prime}
in the Coxeter graph of
L
,

a^{L}_{\alpha,\beta}=0
and
a^{L}_{\alpha^{\prime},\beta}>0
.
Then

B(\omega^{L}_{\beta},\alpha)=\sum_{\gamma\in L}a^{L}_{\gamma,\beta}B(\gamma,\alpha)<0

since
a^{L}_{\gamma,\beta}B(\gamma,\alpha)
is zero for
\gamma=\alpha
, negative for
\gamma=\alpha^{\prime}
and non-positive for all
\gamma\in L\setminus\{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\}
. This contradiction to
B(\omega^{L}_{\beta},\alpha)=\delta_{\beta,\alpha}
completes the proof of (b) if
L
is irreducible.

Now we consider the general situation in which all components of
L
are of finite type. Then
\omega^{F}_{\alpha}
is defined for each component
F
of
L
and each
\alpha\in F
. For
\alpha\in L
, define
\omega_{\alpha}^{L}:=\omega_{\alpha}^{F}
where
F:=L_{\alpha}
. Since the components of
L
are pairwise orthogonal, one has
B(\omega_{\alpha}^{L},\beta)=\delta_{\alpha,\beta}
for all
\beta\in L
.
For fixed
\alpha
, these equations uniquely determine
\alpha^{L}
, since the restriction of
B
to
\operatorname{span}(L)
is non-singular. This proves (a) in general.

From the proof of (a), it follows that in (b), we have
a^{L}_{\alpha,\beta}=0
if
L_{\alpha}\neq L_{\beta}
, and

a^{L}_{\alpha,\beta}=a^{F}_{\alpha,\beta}
if
L_{\alpha}=L_{\beta}=F
. The proof of (b) in general then immediately reduces to the special case already treated, in which
L
is irreducible.

We prove (c). For
\alpha,\beta\in L
, we have

B(\omega_{\alpha}^{L},\omega_{\beta}^{L})=B(\omega_{\alpha}^{L},\sum_{\gamma\in L}a_{\gamma,\beta}^{L}\gamma)=\sum_{\gamma\in L}a_{\gamma,\beta}^{L}B(\omega_{\alpha}^{L},\gamma)=a_{\alpha,\beta}^{L},

by (a). Hence (c) follows from (b).
Part (d) follows directly from (b).
∎

Proposition 6.7
.

Suppose that
L\subseteq\Pi
has only finite-type components and that
V_{L}=V
. Then

(a)

For all
v\in V
,

\pi_{L}(v)=v-\sum_{\alpha\in L}B(v,\alpha)\omega^{L}_{\alpha}

(b)

If
\alpha\in V
, then
\pi_{L}(\alpha)\in\alpha+\operatorname{span}(L)
.

(c)

If
\alpha,\beta\in V
, then
B(\pi_{L}(\alpha),\pi_{L}(\beta))=B(\alpha,\pi_{L}(\beta))
.

(d)

Let
C_{L}:=\{\,v\in V\mid B(v,L)\subseteq\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,\}
denote the fundamental chamber for
W_{L}
on
V
. Then
\operatorname{cone}(\Pi\setminus L)\subseteq-C_{L}
and
\operatorname{cone}(\{\,\omega_{\alpha}^{L}\mid\alpha\in L\,\})=C_{L}\cap\operatorname{cone}(L)
.

(e)

If
\alpha\in-C_{L}
, then
\pi_{L}(\alpha)\in\alpha+(C_{L}\cap\operatorname{cone}(L))
.

(f)

If

\alpha,\beta\in-C_{L}
, then

B(\pi_{L}(\alpha),\pi_{L}(\beta))\leq B(\alpha,\beta)
.

Proof.

Let
v\in V
. Then
v^{\prime}:=\sum_{\alpha\in L}B(v,\alpha)\omega^{L}_{\alpha}\in\operatorname{span}(L)
is defined
and satisfies
B(v^{\prime},\alpha)=B(v,\alpha)
for all
\alpha\in L
.
Hence
v^{\prime\prime}:=v-v^{\prime}\in L^{\perp}
and
v=v^{\prime}+v^{\prime\prime}
, so
\pi_{L}(V)=v^{\prime\prime}
, proving (a). Part (b) follows from (a).
For (c), note that, by (b),
B(\alpha-\pi_{L}(\alpha),\pi_{L}(\beta))\in B(\operatorname{span}(L),L^{\perp})\subseteq\{0\}
.

In (d), the assertion that
\operatorname{cone}(\Pi\setminus L)\subseteq-C_{L}
follows from Theorem
2.9
and the other assertion follows from
Proposition
6.6
(a). Part (e) follows from (a) and (d).

To prove (f), note that by (c) and (e),

B(\pi_{L}(\alpha),\pi_{L}(\beta))-B(\alpha,\beta)=B(\alpha,\pi_{L}(\beta)-\beta)\in B(-C_{L},\operatorname{cone}(L))\subseteq\mathbb{R}_{\leq 0}.\qed

6.8.

For a function
f\colon M\to U
where
M
is a finite set and
U
is a subspace of
V
, we define the Gram determinant

(6.8.1)

{\mathrm{Gram}}(f):=\det((B(f(\alpha),f(\beta)))_{\alpha,\beta\in M})\in\mathbb{R}

By convention, the
0\times 0
matrix has determinant
1
, so
{\mathrm{Gram}}(f):=1
if
M={\emptyset}
.
For any
N\subseteq M
, let
f_{N}\colon N\to U
denote the restriction of
f
to
N
. Thus,
f=f_{M}
.

Suppose that
N
is a subset of
M
such that the restriction of
B
to a bilinear form on
\operatorname{span}(f(N))
is non-singular
(that is,
\operatorname{span}(f(N))\cap(f(N))^{\perp}=\{0\}
).
Then there is a
B
-orthogonal direct sum decomposition

V=\operatorname{span}(f(N))\oplus(f(N))^{\perp}
. We let
p_{N}\colon V\to(f(N))^{\perp}
denote the projection on
(f(N))^{\perp}
corresponding to this decomposition.

Proposition 6.9
.

With notation and assumptions as in the previous subsection,

{\mathrm{Gram}}(f_{M})={\mathrm{Gram}}(f_{N}){\mathrm{Gram}}(p_{N}\circ f_{M\setminus N}).

Proof.

For the proof, suppose without loss of generality that
M=\{1,\ldots,m\}
and
N=\{1,\ldots,n\}
where
n\leq m
.
We may assume that
1\leq n<m
, since otherwise the result holds trivially by the conventions. Write
f(i)=f_{i}
for
i\in M
.
Let
A
be the
m\times m
real matrix with
(i,j)
-entry
a_{i,j}:=B(f_{i},f_{j})
if
1\leq i,j\leq m
. Let
A^{\prime}
be the
m\times m
matrix with
(i,j)
-entry

a^{\prime}_{i,j}:=\begin{cases}B(f_{i},f_{j}),&\text{\rm if $1\leq i,j\leq n$}\\
B(p_{N}(f_{i}),p_{N}(f_{j})),&\text{\rm if $n+1\leq i,j\leq m$}\\
0,&\text{\rm otherwise.}\end{cases}

For
n+1\leq j\leq m
and
1\leq i\leq n
, write

p_{N}(f_{j})=\sum_{i=1}^{m}c_{i,j}f_{i}
where
c_{i,j}\in\mathbb{R}
, and define
c_{i,j}:=\delta_{i,j}
if
n+1\leq i\leq m
. For
1\leq j\leq m
and
1\leq i\leq n
, let
c_{i,j}:=\delta_{i,j}
.
Let
C
denote the
m\times m
matrix with
(i,j)
-entry
c_{i,j}
.
A simple computation suing bilinearity of
B
shows that

A^{\prime}=CAC^{\mathrm{t}}
where
C^{\mathrm{t}}
denotes the transpose of
C
. We have
\det(C)=1
since
C
is upper unitriangular, so

{\mathrm{Gram}}(f_{M})=\det(A)=\det(A^{\prime})={\mathrm{Gram}}(f_{N}){\mathrm{Gram}}(p_{N}f_{M\setminus N})

since
\det(a^{\prime}_{ij})_{1\leq i,j\leq n}={\mathrm{Gram}}(f_{N}
,

\det(a^{\prime}_{ij})_{n+i\leq i,j\leq m}={\mathrm{Gram}}(f_{N})
, and
a^{\prime}_{ij}=0
if
i\leq n
and
j>n
, or if
i>n
and
j\leq n
.
∎

Proposition 6.10
.

Let
J\in\operatorname{ob}(G)
and
U{\,\supseteq\,}W_{J}
be a reflection subgroup of
W
. Then
\Pi_{U}{\,\supseteq\,}J
.

(a)

Let
\alpha,\beta\in\Pi_{U}\setminus J
. Then
B_{J}(\pi_{J}(\alpha),\pi_{J}(\beta))\leq B(\alpha,\beta)
where if
\alpha\neq\beta
, then
B(\alpha,\beta)\leq 0
.

(b)

Let
\alpha\in\Pi_{U}\setminus J
and
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{\alpha\}
denote the component of
\Pi_{U}
containing
\alpha
, where
L\subseteq J
. Then
L
is of finite type and, letting
\alpha^{\prime}:=\pi_{J}(\alpha)
, we have

\begin{cases}B_{J}(\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})>0,&\text{\rm if $L^{\prime}$ is of finite type}\\
B_{J}(\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})=0,&\text{\rm if $L^{\prime}$ is of affine type}\\
B_{J}(\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})<0,&\text{\rm otherwise i.e. if $L^{\prime}$ is of indefinite type.}\\
\end{cases}

(c)

If
\Pi_{U}\setminus J
is finite and
L
is a finite union of components of
J
which contains all roots joined to a root in
\Pi_{U}\setminus J
, then
\det(B_{J}(\pi_{J}(\alpha),\pi_{J}(\beta)))_{\alpha,\beta\in\Pi_{U}\setminus J}
has the same sign (
+
,
-
or
0
) as
\det(B(\alpha,\beta))_{\alpha,\beta\in(\Pi_{U}\setminus J)\cup L}
.

Proof.

Part (a) holds by Proposition
6.7
(f) since
\Pi_{U}\setminus J\subseteq-C_{J}
by Theorem
2.9
.

We prove (b). Recall
6.4
. Note
K:=\{\,\beta\in J\mid B(\alpha,\beta)\neq 0\,\}
is finite. Since
L
is the union of the components of
J
which contain an element of
K
,
L
is a spherical subset of
\Pi
. Note that
\alpha^{\prime}=\pi_{J}(\alpha)=\pi_{L}(\alpha)\in\operatorname{span}(L^{\prime})
since
\alpha\in L^{\prime}
and
L^{\prime}\perp(J\setminus L)
.
Let
f:L^{\prime}\to V
be the inclusion.
By Proposition
6.9
, we have

{\mathrm{Gram}}(f_{L^{\prime}})={\mathrm{Gram}}(f_{L})B_{J}(\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})
. Since
L
is spherical,
{\mathrm{Gram}}(f_{L})>0
. Now by
[
32
, Ch 4]
,
L^{\prime}
is of finite (resp., affine) type if and only if the restriction of
B
to
\operatorname{span}(L^{\prime})
is positive definite (resp., positive semidefinite of corank one). Since the restriction of

B
to
\operatorname{span}(L)
is positive definite, it follows that
L^{\prime}
is of finite (resp., affine) type if and only if
B_{J}(\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})
is positive (resp., zero). Part (b) follows.

The proof of (c) is similar to that of (b). We have

\pi_{J}(\alpha)=\pi_{L}(\alpha)
for all
\alpha\in\Pi_{U}\setminus J
,
and
L
is spherical.
Let
f\colon(\Pi_{U}\setminus J)\cup L\to V
be the inclusion. Then
Proposition
6.9
implies that

{\mathrm{Gram}}(f)={\mathrm{Gram}}(f_{L}){\mathrm{Gram}}(\pi_{L}\circ f_{\Pi_{U}\setminus J})
. Since
L
is
spherical,
{\mathrm{Gram}}(f_{L})>0
. Then (c) follows since

{\mathrm{Gram}}(f)=\det((B(\alpha,\beta))_{\alpha,\beta\in(\Pi_{U}\setminus J)\cup L})
and
{\mathrm{Gram}}(\pi_{L}\circ f_{\Pi_{U}\setminus J})=\det((B(\pi_{J}(\alpha),\pi_{J}(\beta)))_{\alpha,\beta\in\Pi_{U}\setminus J})
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
1.58
.

Part (a) is clear from the discussion above and Proposition
1.50
.

We prove (b). Let
\alpha,\beta\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-6.18059pt\kern 6.56947pt}}_{{\kern-9.86899pt{J}\kern 6.56947pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-6.18059pt\kern 6.56947pt}}_{{\kern-9.86899pt{J}\kern 6.56947pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-3.69446pt\kern 4.08334pt}}_{{\kern-6.47188pt{J}\kern 4.08334pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-2.52779pt\kern 2.91667pt}}_{{\kern-5.3052pt{J}\kern 2.91667pt}}}
.
Write
\alpha=\pi_{J}(\alpha^{\prime})
and
\beta=\pi_{J}(\beta^{\prime})
where
\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}\in\Pi\setminus J
. Proposition
6.10
(a)–(b) show that
B_{J}(\alpha,\beta)>0
if and only if
\alpha^{\prime}=\beta^{\prime}
and
the component
L^{\prime}
of
J\cup\{\alpha^{\prime}\}
containing
\alpha^{\prime}
is of finite type. But
L^{\prime}
is of finite type if and only if
\alpha\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-6.18059pt\kern 6.56947pt}}_{{\kern-9.86899pt{J}\kern 6.56947pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-6.18059pt\kern 6.56947pt}}_{{\kern-9.86899pt{J}\kern 6.56947pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-3.69446pt\kern 4.08334pt}}_{{\kern-6.47188pt{J}\kern 4.08334pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-2.52779pt\kern 2.91667pt}}_{{\kern-5.3052pt{J}\kern 2.91667pt}}}^{{\mathrm{re}}}
, by Theorem
1.33
and Proposition
1.35
. Part (b) follows. Part (c) also follows using Proposition
6.10
(c), and (d) follows from (c) using
Theorem
1.33
and Proposition
1.35
.

In (e), it is sufficient to prove the two unions there are of disjoint sets. Disjointness in the first union holds for any signed groupoid set, and that in the second union follows since
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-6.18059pt\kern 6.56947pt}}_{{\kern-9.86899pt{J}\kern 6.56947pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-6.18059pt\kern 6.56947pt}}_{{\kern-9.86899pt{J}\kern 6.56947pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-3.69446pt\kern 4.08334pt}}_{{\kern-6.47188pt{J}\kern 4.08334pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-2.52779pt\kern 2.91667pt}}_{{\kern-5.3052pt{J}\kern 2.91667pt}}}\subseteq{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}}_{X}
.
Part (f) follows from Proposition
1.50
(e). ∎

Corollary 6.11
.

Let
J\in\operatorname{ob}(G)
and let
\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}\in\Pi\setminus J

with
\pi_{J}(\alpha^{\prime})=\pi_{J}(\beta^{\prime})\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-6.18059pt\kern 6.56947pt}}_{{\kern-9.86899pt{J}\kern 6.56947pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-6.18059pt\kern 6.56947pt}}_{{\kern-9.86899pt{J}\kern 6.56947pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-3.69446pt\kern 4.08334pt}}_{{\kern-6.47188pt{J}\kern 4.08334pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-2.52779pt\kern 2.91667pt}}_{{\kern-5.3052pt{J}\kern 2.91667pt}}}^{{\mathrm{re}}}
. Then
\alpha^{\prime}=\beta^{\prime}
.

Proof.

If instead
\alpha^{\prime}\neq\beta^{\prime}
, the proof of Proposition
1.58
(b) above would show that

0\geq B_{J}(\pi_{J}(\alpha^{\prime}),\pi_{J}(\beta^{\prime}))>0.\qed

Example 6.12
.

We give an example to show that, if
\Pi
is linearly dependent, the above corollary does not hold in general if
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-6.18059pt\kern 6.56947pt}}_{{\kern-9.86899pt{J}\kern 6.56947pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-6.18059pt\kern 6.56947pt}}_{{\kern-9.86899pt{J}\kern 6.56947pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-3.69446pt\kern 4.08334pt}}_{{\kern-6.47188pt{J}\kern 4.08334pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-2.52779pt\kern 2.91667pt}}_{{\kern-5.3052pt{J}\kern 2.91667pt}}}^{{\mathrm{re}}}
is replaced by
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-6.18059pt\kern 6.56947pt}}_{{\kern-9.86899pt{J}\kern 6.56947pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-6.18059pt\kern 6.56947pt}}_{{\kern-9.86899pt{J}\kern 6.56947pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-3.69446pt\kern 4.08334pt}}_{{\kern-6.47188pt{J}\kern 4.08334pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-2.52779pt\kern 2.91667pt}}_{{\kern-5.3052pt{J}\kern 2.91667pt}}}
, and

({\mathcal{V}},\iota)
does not in general afford a realization (only a weak realization) of
(G,\Upsilon_{P})
.

Let
V
be a three-dimensional real vector space with basis

\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3}
and let

\alpha_{4}:=\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}-\alpha_{1}\in V
. Let
B
denote the symmetric
bilinear form on
V
such that the matrix

(2B(\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j}))_{1\leq i,j\leq 4}
is the symmetric generalized Cartan matrix

\begin{bmatrix}[r]2&-1&0&-3\\
-1&2&-3&0\\
0&-3&2&-1\\
-3&0&-1&2\end{bmatrix}.

By bilinearity of
B
, the last row and column of this matrix are determined by the upper left
3\times 3
submatrix and the definition of
\alpha_{4}
.

Let
\Pi=\{\,\alpha_{i}\mid 1\leq i\leq 4\,\}
. Let
s_{i}
denote the reflection
s_{i}:=s_{\alpha_{i}}
on
V
,

S:=\{\,s_{i}\mid 1\leq i\leq 4\,\}
,
W=\langle\,S\,\rangle\subseteq\mathrm{O}(V)
and
\Phi:=\{\,w\alpha\mid w\in W,\alpha\in\Pi\,\}
. Then
(W,S)
is a Coxeter system and
(\Phi,\Pi)
is a based root system of
(W,S)
in the quadratic space
(V,B)
.
The Coxeter graph and presentation graph (see
2.23
) of
(W,S)
, regarded as graphs with vertex set
\Pi
, are respectively

\textstyle{{\alpha_{3}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\infty}
\textstyle{{\alpha_{4}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\infty}
\textstyle{{\alpha_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\alpha_{2},}}

\textstyle{{\alpha_{3}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{3}
\textstyle{{\alpha_{4}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\alpha_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{3}
\textstyle{{\alpha_{2}.}}

Note that
\operatorname{cone}(\Pi)
is a polyhedral cone, with the elements of
\Pi
as representatives of the extreme rays of the cone. In fact, the convex polygon
P
with vertices
\alpha_{i}
for
i=1,\ldots,4
is a quadrilateral cross-section of the cone. Note that
P
must be as diagrammed at the right above since

\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{4})=\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3})
.

Corollary
2.23
explains why the presentation graph is a subgraph of the edge graph of the quadrilateral
P
, or equivalently, why the edge of the Coxeter graph joining
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{4}
(respectively,

\alpha_{2}
and
\alpha_{3}
) has label
\infty
.

Let
J:=\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\}
, which is a spherical subset of type
A_{2}
of
\Pi
, and let
G
be the component containing
J
of the full Brink-Howlett groupoid of
(W,S)
. Then
G
has only one object,
J
, and one morphism.
The conditions
6.4
(i)–(iii) hold. It is straightforward to check
\pi_{J}(\alpha_{3})=\alpha_{3}+2\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{1}
.
By symmetry, we have
\pi_{J}(\alpha_{4})=\alpha_{4}+2\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}
. Hence
\pi_{J}(\alpha_{4})=\pi_{J}(\alpha_{3})
. By Corollary
6.11
,

\pi_{J}(\alpha_{3})=\pi_{J}(\alpha_{4})\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-6.18059pt\kern 6.56947pt}}_{{\kern-9.86899pt{J}\kern 6.56947pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-6.18059pt\kern 6.56947pt}}_{{\kern-9.86899pt{J}\kern 6.56947pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-3.69446pt\kern 4.08334pt}}_{{\kern-6.47188pt{J}\kern 4.08334pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-2.52779pt\kern 2.91667pt}}_{{\kern-5.3052pt{J}\kern 2.91667pt}}}^{{\mathrm{im}}}
. Since

U_{i}:=W_{J\cup\{\alpha_{i}\}}\in P_{J}
for
i=3,4
are distinct
with
r_{U_{i},J}=\alpha_{i}
, it follows that
(\nu,\iota)
is not a realization of
(G,\Upsilon_{P})
in this example.

Proof of Proposition
1.60
.

We prove (a). Let
K\subseteq\Pi\setminus J
.
Write the union of all components of
K\cup J
which intersect
K
as
L\dot{\cup}K
. Note
L\subseteq J
is the union of all
components of
J
which contain a vertex which is joined to a vertex of
K
in the Coxeter graph of
\Pi
. Recall that

\nu(K,J)
exists if and only
L\cup K
is spherical, in which case
K\cup L
is linearly independent.

If
\nu(K,J)
exists, then
K
is certainly finite. So there is no loss of generality in assuming for the proof of (a) that
K
is finite. Since
K\perp J\setminus L
, we have

\pi_{J}(K)=\pi_{L}(K)\subseteq\operatorname{span}(K\cup L)
. Since
L
is spherical, the restriction of

B
to
\operatorname{span}(L)
is positive definite and there is a
B
-orthogonal direct sum
\operatorname{span}(K\cup L)=\operatorname{span}(L)\oplus\operatorname{span}(\pi_{L}(K))
. It follows that the
restriction of
B
to
\operatorname{span}(K\cup L)
is positive definite (i.e.
K\cup L
is spherical) if and only if the restriction of
B
(or
equivalently, of
B_{J}
) to
\operatorname{span}(\pi_{L}(K))=\operatorname{span}(\pi_{J}(K))
is positive definite. This proves (a).

For the proof of (b)–(f), assume that
w:=\nu(K,J)
exists, and let
L
be as in the proof of (a). Since
K\cup L
is spherical, it is linearly independent. From the proof of (a),

\begin{split}|L|+|K|&=\dim(\operatorname{span}(L\cup K))=\dim(\operatorname{span}(L))+\dim(\operatorname{span}(\pi_{L}(K)))\\
&=|L|+\dim(\operatorname{span}(\pi_{L}(K))).\end{split}

Hence
\dim(\operatorname{span}(\pi_{J}(K)))=\dim(\operatorname{span}(\pi_{J}(K)))=|K|
, so
\pi_{J}(K)
is linearly independent, proving (b).

We prove (c). We have
w=\nu(K,J)=\nu(K,L)=w_{K\cup L}w_{L}
and
\nu(\alpha,J)=\nu(\alpha,L)=w_{L\cup\{\alpha\}}w_{L}
for
\alpha\in K
. We have to show that

\bigvee_{\alpha\in K}w_{L}w_{L\cup\{\alpha\}}=w_{L}w_{K\cup L}
in weak right order of
W
. This follows from

\bigvee_{\alpha\in K}w_{L\cup\{\alpha\}}=w_{K\cup L}

since each element of
W
appearing in this formula is greater than or equal to
w_{L}
in weak right order.

For (d)–(f), refer to Lemma
1.25
. First, (d) here is just a reformulation of Lemma
1.25
(d).
To prove (e), note that

M=L\,\dot{\cup}\,K
is spherical from above,
w=w_{M}w_{L}
,
J^{\prime}=w(J)=(J\setminus L)\dot{\cup}-w_{M}(L)
and

K^{\prime}=((J\setminus L)\cup M)\setminus((J\setminus L)\cup-w_{M}(L))=M\setminus-w_{M}(L)=-w_{M}(K),

since
M=L\,\dot{\cup}\,K
and
w_{M}(M)=-M
. Hence the map
\sigma
defined in (e) is a bijection as stated.
Finally, for
\alpha\in K
, we have

w(\alpha_{J})=w(\pi_{J}(\alpha))=\pi_{J^{\prime}}(w\alpha)
.
We have

w\alpha=w_{M}w_{L}(\alpha)\in w_{M}(\alpha+\operatorname{span}(L))=-\sigma(\alpha)+\operatorname{span}(w_{M}(L))

where, since
w_{L}L=-L
,

\operatorname{span}(w_{M}(L)=\operatorname{span}(w_{M}w_{L}(L))\subseteq\operatorname{span}(w(J))=\operatorname{span}(J^{\prime})\subseteq\ker(\pi_{J^{\prime}}).

Hence
w(\alpha_{J})=(-\sigma\alpha)_{J^{\prime}}
and (f) follows from (e) since the map
\beta\mapsto\pi_{J^{\prime}}(\beta)\colon K^{\prime}\to\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J^{\prime}}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-6.18059pt\kern 6.56947pt}}_{{\kern-11.33148pt{J^{\prime}}\kern 6.56947pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J^{\prime}}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-6.18059pt\kern 6.56947pt}}_{{\kern-11.33148pt{J^{\prime}}\kern 6.56947pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J^{\prime}}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-3.69446pt\kern 4.08334pt}}_{{\kern-7.93437pt{J^{\prime}}\kern 4.08334pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J^{\prime}}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-2.52779pt\kern 2.91667pt}}_{{\kern-6.7677pt{J^{\prime}}\kern 2.91667pt}}}^{{\mathrm{re}}}
is injective by Corollary
6.11
.
Alternatively, one could finish by noting that by symmetry, there is a map

-\beta_{J^{\prime}}\mapsto(\sigma^{-1}\beta)_{J}\colon-\Pi_{J^{\prime}}(K^{\prime})\to\Pi_{J}(K)
and that it is an inverse to the map in (f).
∎

6.13.

The following proposition records some basic facts about “non-reduced” (weakly) realized root systems.

Proposition 6.14
.

Let
J\in\operatorname{ob}(G)
,
U,U^{\prime}\in R_{J}
and

U^{\prime\prime}\in M_{J}
.

(a)

Assume
U\subseteq U^{\prime}
. Then
r^{\prime}_{U,J}=cr^{\prime}_{U^{\prime},J}
for some
c\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 1}
.

(b)

Suppose that
r^{\prime}_{U^{\prime},J}=cr^{\prime}_{U,J}
with
c\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
and that
(U,+)\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-5.75003pt\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-9.43843pt{J}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-5.75003pt\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-9.43843pt{J}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-3.42223pt\kern 3.81111pt}}_{{\kern-6.19965pt{J}\kern 3.81111pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-2.33334pt\kern 2.72223pt}}_{{\kern-5.11076pt{J}\kern 2.72223pt}}}^{{\mathrm{re}}}_{R}
. Then
(U^{\prime},+)\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-5.75003pt\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-9.43843pt{J}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-5.75003pt\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-9.43843pt{J}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-3.42223pt\kern 3.81111pt}}_{{\kern-6.19965pt{J}\kern 3.81111pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-2.33334pt\kern 2.72223pt}}_{{\kern-5.11076pt{J}\kern 2.72223pt}}}^{{\mathrm{re}}}_{R}
.

(c)

Assume that
r^{\prime}_{U,J}=cr^{\prime}_{U^{\prime\prime},J}
where
c>0
and both

(U,+)
and
(U^{\prime\prime},+)
are elements of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-5.75003pt\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-9.43843pt{J}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-5.75003pt\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-9.43843pt{J}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-3.42223pt\kern 3.81111pt}}_{{\kern-6.19965pt{J}\kern 3.81111pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-2.33334pt\kern 2.72223pt}}_{{\kern-5.11076pt{J}\kern 2.72223pt}}}^{{\mathrm{re}}}_{R}
. Then
U\subseteq U^{\prime\prime}
.

(d)

Suppose that
(U,+)\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-5.75003pt\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-9.43843pt{J}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-5.75003pt\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-9.43843pt{J}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-3.42223pt\kern 3.81111pt}}_{{\kern-6.19965pt{J}\kern 3.81111pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-2.33334pt\kern 2.72223pt}}_{{\kern-5.11076pt{J}\kern 2.72223pt}}}^{{\mathrm{re}}}_{R}
, and that

U\subseteq U^{\prime\prime}
. Write, by (a),
r^{\prime}_{U,J}=cr^{\prime}_{U^{\prime\prime},J}
where
c\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 1}
. Then
c=1
if and only if
U^{\prime\prime}=U
.

(e)

For any root
\alpha\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}_{R}^{{\mathrm{re}},+}
, there are only finitely many
c\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
such that

c\alpha\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}_{R}^{{\mathrm{re}},+}
.

Proof.

We prove (a). Since
U\subseteq U^{\prime}
, we have
\Phi_{U}\subseteq\Phi_{U^{\prime}}
, and thus each element of
\Pi_{U}=J\cup\{r_{U,J}\}
can be expressed as a non-negative linear combination of
\Pi_{U^{\prime}}=J\cup\{r_{U^{\prime},J}\}
. Hence

r_{U,J}\in cr_{U^{\prime},J}+\operatorname{cone}(J)

where
c\geq 0
. We must have
c>0
. For suppose that
c=0
. Then
\Pi_{U}\subseteq\operatorname{span}(J)
, which implies that
\Pi_{U}\subseteq\Phi_{J}
by Proposition
2.30
and so
U\subseteq W_{J}
, contrary to
U\in R_{J}
. By
[
7
, Proposition 2.26]
, we actually have
c\geq 1
. Applying
\pi_{J}
to
r_{U,J}\in cr_{U^{\prime},J}+\operatorname{cone}(J)
shows

r^{\prime}_{U,J}\in cr^{\prime}_{U^{\prime},J}+\pi_{J}(\operatorname{cone}(J))
.
Since
J\subseteq\ker(\pi_{J})
, (a) follows.

For the proof of (b), suppose that
r^{\prime}_{U,J}=cr^{\prime}_{U^{\prime},J}
where
c>0
and
(U,+)\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-5.75003pt\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-9.43843pt{J}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-5.75003pt\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-9.43843pt{J}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-3.42223pt\kern 3.81111pt}}_{{\kern-6.19965pt{J}\kern 3.81111pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-2.33334pt\kern 2.72223pt}}_{{\kern-5.11076pt{J}\kern 2.72223pt}}}^{{\mathrm{re}},+}_{R}
. There is a morphism
g=(K,w,J)
in
G
such that
(\Upsilon_{R}(g))(U,+)\in\Upsilon^{-}_{R}(K)
. Using Proposition
1.58
(a) shows in turn that
wr^{\prime}_{U,J}\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-10.17876pt{K}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-10.17876pt{K}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.88959pt{K}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.88959pt{K}\kern 5.55557pt}}}_{R}^{-}\subseteq-\operatorname{cone}(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-6.18059pt\kern 6.56947pt}}_{{\kern-11.19266pt{K}\kern 6.56947pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-6.18059pt\kern 6.56947pt}}_{{\kern-11.19266pt{K}\kern 6.56947pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-3.69446pt\kern 4.08334pt}}_{{\kern-7.41736pt{K}\kern 4.08334pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-2.52779pt\kern 2.91667pt}}_{{\kern-6.25069pt{K}\kern 2.91667pt}}})
, that

wr^{\prime}_{U^{\prime},J}=cwr^{\prime}_{U,J}\in-\operatorname{cone}(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-6.18059pt\kern 6.56947pt}}_{{\kern-11.19266pt{K}\kern 6.56947pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-6.18059pt\kern 6.56947pt}}_{{\kern-11.19266pt{K}\kern 6.56947pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-3.69446pt\kern 4.08334pt}}_{{\kern-7.41736pt{K}\kern 4.08334pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-2.52779pt\kern 2.91667pt}}_{{\kern-6.25069pt{K}\kern 2.91667pt}}})
,
that
wr^{\prime}_{U^{\prime},J}\not\in\operatorname{cone}(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-6.18059pt\kern 6.56947pt}}_{{\kern-11.19266pt{K}\kern 6.56947pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-6.18059pt\kern 6.56947pt}}_{{\kern-11.19266pt{K}\kern 6.56947pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-3.69446pt\kern 4.08334pt}}_{{\kern-7.41736pt{K}\kern 4.08334pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-2.52779pt\kern 2.91667pt}}_{{\kern-6.25069pt{K}\kern 2.91667pt}}})
,
that
wr^{\prime}_{U^{\prime},J}\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-10.17876pt{K}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-10.17876pt{K}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.88959pt{K}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.88959pt{K}\kern 5.55557pt}}}_{R}^{-}
and hence that
(\Upsilon_{R}(g))(U^{\prime},+)\in\Upsilon^{-}_{R}(K)
and
(U^{\prime},+)\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-5.75003pt\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-9.43843pt{J}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-5.75003pt\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-9.43843pt{J}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-3.42223pt\kern 3.81111pt}}_{{\kern-6.19965pt{J}\kern 3.81111pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-2.33334pt\kern 2.72223pt}}_{{\kern-5.11076pt{J}\kern 2.72223pt}}}^{{\mathrm{re}},+}_{R}
.

We prove (c). Note that
U\in N_{J}
and
U^{\prime\prime}\in Q_{J}
.
We have
r_{U,J}\in cr_{U^{\prime\prime},J}+\operatorname{span}(J)\subseteq\operatorname{span}(\Pi_{U^{\prime\prime}})
since
\Pi_{U^{\prime\prime}}=J\cup\{r_{U^{\prime\prime},J}\}
. Note
U^{\prime\prime}
is a parabolic subgroup of
W
with all its components of finite type. By applying Proposition
2.30
to a standard parabolic subgroup which is
W
-conjugate
to
U^{\prime\prime}
, it follows that
r_{U,J}\in\Phi_{U^{\prime\prime}}
. Hence
\Pi_{U}=J\cup\{r_{U,J}\}\subseteq\Pi_{U^{\prime\prime}}
and so
U\subseteq U^{\prime\prime}
as required.

Now we prove (d). The assumptions imply that
U\in N_{J}
and
U^{\prime\prime}\in Q_{J}\subseteq N_{J}
.
Clearly,
c=1
if
U^{\prime\prime}=U
. On the other hand, if
c=1
, then

r^{\prime}_{U,J}=r^{\prime}_{U^{\prime\prime},J}
, which implies
U=U^{\prime\prime}
by Theorem

1.49
(d).

Finally, we prove (e). We may assume without loss of generality
by (a)–(d) that
\alpha=r^{\prime}_{U^{\prime\prime},J}
where
U^{\prime\prime}\in Q_{J}
, so
[U^{\prime\prime}:W_{J}]
is finite. By (c), it suffices to prove there are only finitely many
U\in R_{J}
such that
U\subseteq U^{\prime\prime}
. Any such
U
satisfies
W_{J}\subseteq U\subseteq U^{\prime\prime}
, and is therefore the union of some subset of the finitely many
left (say) cosets of
W_{J}
in
U^{\prime\prime}
, so there are only finitely many possibilities for
U
. ∎

Corollary 6.15
.

Let
J\in\operatorname{ob}(G)
. Then
the parallelism class of a root
\alpha\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}^{{\mathrm{re}}}_{X}
is
\mathbb{R}_{>0}\alpha\cap\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}_{X}
, which is a finite set.

Proof.

Clearly, any positive multiple of
\alpha
is in the same parallelism class as
\alpha
. Consider any roots
\alpha_{i}\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}^{{\mathrm{re}}}_{X}
for
i=1,2
. We have
\alpha_{i}=\epsilon_{i}r_{U_{i},J}
for some
U_{i}\in X_{J}
and
\epsilon_{i}\in\{\pm 1\}
. Since
\alpha_{i}
is a real root, we also have
U_{i}\in N_{J}
. By Theorem
1.43
, there exists unique

U_{i}^{\prime\prime}\in Q_{J}\subseteq P_{J}\subseteq X_{J}
such that
U_{i}\subseteq U_{i}^{\prime\prime}
. Then
U_{i}^{\prime\prime}\in P_{J}\subseteq M_{J}
. Proposition
6.14
implies
r_{U_{i},J}=c_{i}r_{U_{i}^{\prime\prime},J}
for some scalar
c_{i}\geq 1
. Let

\beta_{i}:=(r_{U_{i}^{\prime\prime},J},\epsilon_{i})\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-5.75003pt\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-9.43843pt{J}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-5.75003pt\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-9.43843pt{J}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-3.42223pt\kern 3.81111pt}}_{{\kern-6.19965pt{J}\kern 3.81111pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-2.33334pt\kern 2.72223pt}}_{{\kern-5.11076pt{J}\kern 2.72223pt}}}_{Q}\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-5.75003pt\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-9.43843pt{J}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-5.75003pt\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-9.43843pt{J}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-3.42223pt\kern 3.81111pt}}_{{\kern-6.19965pt{J}\kern 3.81111pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-2.33334pt\kern 2.72223pt}}_{{\kern-5.11076pt{J}\kern 2.72223pt}}}_{X}^{{\mathrm{re}}}
.
By Theorem
1.49
,
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}
are in the same parallelism class
in
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}^{{\mathrm{re}}}_{X}
if and only if

\beta_{1},\beta_{2}
are in the same parallelism class in

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-5.75003pt\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-9.43843pt{J}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-5.75003pt\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-9.43843pt{J}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-3.42223pt\kern 3.81111pt}}_{{\kern-6.19965pt{J}\kern 3.81111pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-2.33334pt\kern 2.72223pt}}_{{\kern-5.11076pt{J}\kern 2.72223pt}}}_{X}^{{\mathrm{re}}}
, and hence if and only if
\beta_{1}
and
\beta_{2}
are in the same parallelism class in
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-5.75003pt\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-9.43843pt{J}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-5.75003pt\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-9.43843pt{J}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-3.42223pt\kern 3.81111pt}}_{{\kern-6.19965pt{J}\kern 3.81111pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-2.33334pt\kern 2.72223pt}}_{{\kern-5.11076pt{J}\kern 2.72223pt}}}_{Q}
(recall from Theorem
1.33
that
(G,\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-5.75003pt\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-9.43843pt{J}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-5.75003pt\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-9.43843pt{J}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-3.42223pt\kern 3.81111pt}}_{{\kern-6.19965pt{J}\kern 3.81111pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-2.33334pt\kern 2.72223pt}}_{{\kern-5.11076pt{J}\kern 2.72223pt}}}_{Q})\subseteq(G,\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-5.75003pt\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-9.43843pt{J}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-5.75003pt\kern 6.13892pt}}_{{\kern-9.43843pt{J}\kern 6.13892pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-3.42223pt\kern 3.81111pt}}_{{\kern-6.19965pt{J}\kern 3.81111pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Upsilon^{{\kern-2.33334pt\kern 2.72223pt}}_{{\kern-5.11076pt{J}\kern 2.72223pt}}}_{X})
).

By Theorem
1.22
, the parallelism classes of roots
of
(G,\Lambda^{{\mathrm{rec}}})
are all singleton sets. By Theorem

1.33
, so are those of (the isomorphic signed
G
-set)
(G,\Upsilon_{Q})
.
Hence if
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}
are in the same parallelism class of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}^{{\mathrm{re}}}_{X}
, then
\beta_{1}=\beta_{2}

and so
\alpha_{2}=c_{2}c_{1}^{-1}\alpha_{1}\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}\alpha_{1}
as required. The final assertion, on finiteness of parallelism classes, holds by Proposition
6.14
(e).
∎

Proof of Proposition
1.63
.

From Proposition
1.60
, we have

(6.15.1)

\nu(\alpha,J)\alpha_{J}=-\alpha^{\prime}_{J^{\prime}}

where

(6.15.2)

B_{J}(\alpha_{J},\alpha_{J})=B_{J^{\prime}}(\alpha^{\prime}_{J^{\prime}},\alpha^{\prime}_{J^{\prime}})\neq 0.

Write
L:=J\cup\{\alpha\}=J^{\prime}\cup\{\alpha^{\prime}\}
.
Let
M\subseteq J
be such that
M\cup\{\alpha\}
is the component of
L=J\cup\{\alpha\}
which contains
\alpha
.
Observe for subsequent use that the conditions on
F
in
6.5
all hold if
F
is any one of
J
,
L
,
M
or
M\cup\{\alpha\}
.

There is is a
B
-orthogonal direct sum decomposition

V=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{L}}}}V^{{\kern-6.05559pt\kern 6.44447pt}}_{{\kern-9.8903pt{L}\kern 6.44447pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{L}}}}V^{{\kern-6.05559pt\kern 6.44447pt}}_{{\kern-9.8903pt{L}\kern 6.44447pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{L}}}}V^{{\kern-3.55832pt\kern 3.9472pt}}_{{\kern-6.44026pt{L}\kern 3.9472pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{L}}}}V^{{\kern-2.43054pt\kern 2.81943pt}}_{{\kern-5.31248pt{L}\kern 2.81943pt}}}\oplus\operatorname{span}(L)
where
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{L}}}}V^{{\kern-6.05559pt\kern 6.44447pt}}_{{\kern-9.8903pt{L}\kern 6.44447pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{L}}}}V^{{\kern-6.05559pt\kern 6.44447pt}}_{{\kern-9.8903pt{L}\kern 6.44447pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{L}}}}V^{{\kern-3.55832pt\kern 3.9472pt}}_{{\kern-6.44026pt{L}\kern 3.9472pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{L}}}}V^{{\kern-2.43054pt\kern 2.81943pt}}_{{\kern-5.31248pt{L}\kern 2.81943pt}}}=L^{\perp}
, as described in
6.5
. We therefore have an orthogonal direct sum decomposition

(6.15.3)

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}V^{{\kern-6.05559pt\kern 6.44447pt}}_{{\kern-9.74399pt{J}\kern 6.44447pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}V^{{\kern-6.05559pt\kern 6.44447pt}}_{{\kern-9.74399pt{J}\kern 6.44447pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}V^{{\kern-3.55832pt\kern 3.9472pt}}_{{\kern-6.33574pt{J}\kern 3.9472pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}V^{{\kern-2.43054pt\kern 2.81943pt}}_{{\kern-5.20796pt{J}\kern 2.81943pt}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{L}}}}V^{{\kern-6.05559pt\kern 6.44447pt}}_{{\kern-9.8903pt{L}\kern 6.44447pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{L}}}}V^{{\kern-6.05559pt\kern 6.44447pt}}_{{\kern-9.8903pt{L}\kern 6.44447pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{L}}}}V^{{\kern-3.55832pt\kern 3.9472pt}}_{{\kern-6.44026pt{L}\kern 3.9472pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{L}}}}V^{{\kern-2.43054pt\kern 2.81943pt}}_{{\kern-5.31248pt{L}\kern 2.81943pt}}}\oplus\operatorname{span}(\alpha_{J}).

In fact, for
v\in V
, we have
v=v_{J}
if
v\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}V^{{\kern-6.05559pt\kern 6.44447pt}}_{{\kern-9.74399pt{J}\kern 6.44447pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}V^{{\kern-6.05559pt\kern 6.44447pt}}_{{\kern-9.74399pt{J}\kern 6.44447pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}V^{{\kern-3.55832pt\kern 3.9472pt}}_{{\kern-6.33574pt{J}\kern 3.9472pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}V^{{\kern-2.43054pt\kern 2.81943pt}}_{{\kern-5.20796pt{J}\kern 2.81943pt}}}
, and in general,

(6.15.4)

v_{J}=v_{L}+c\alpha_{J}\qquad c:=\frac{B_{J}(v_{J},\alpha_{J})}{B_{J}(\alpha_{J},\alpha_{J})}

From the analogous formula for
v_{J^{\prime}}
, it follows that for all
v\in V
,

(6.15.5)

v_{J}-\frac{B_{J}(v_{J},\alpha_{J})}{B_{J}(\alpha_{J},\alpha_{J})}\alpha_{J}=v_{L}=v_{J^{\prime}}-\frac{B_{J^{\prime}}(v_{J^{\prime}},\alpha^{\prime}_{J^{\prime}})}{B_{J^{\prime}}(\alpha^{\prime}_{J^{\prime}},\alpha^{\prime}_{J^{\prime}})}\alpha^{\prime}_{J^{\prime}}.

Using this, it follows there is linear isometry
\tau_{g}\colon\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.06354pt{J}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.01353pt{J}\kern 2.625pt}}}\to\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J^{\prime}}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-10.66483pt{J^{\prime}}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J^{\prime}}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-10.66483pt{J^{\prime}}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J^{\prime}}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-7.52603pt{J^{\prime}}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J^{\prime}}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-6.47603pt{J^{\prime}}\kern 2.625pt}}}
as stated in Proposition
1.63
(a), given explicitly by

(6.15.6)

\tau_{g}(v)=v-\frac{B_{J}(v,\alpha_{J})}{B_{J}(\alpha_{J},\alpha_{J})}\alpha_{J}+\frac{B_{J}(v,\alpha_{J})}{B_{J}(\alpha_{J},\alpha_{J})}\alpha^{\prime}_{J^{\prime}},\qquad v\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.06354pt{J}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.01353pt{J}\kern 2.625pt}}}.

Saying
\tau_{g}
is a linear isometry means that it is a
\mathbb{R}
-linear map satisfying

(6.15.7)

B_{J^{\prime}}(\tau_{g}(v),\tau_{g}(v^{\prime}))=B_{J}(v,v^{\prime}),\qquad v,v^{\prime}\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.06354pt{J}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.01353pt{J}\kern 2.625pt}}}.

The orthogonal reflection
s_{\alpha_{J}}\colon\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.06354pt{J}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.01353pt{J}\kern 2.625pt}}}\to\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.06354pt{J}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.01353pt{J}\kern 2.625pt}}}
in
\alpha_{J}
on the quadratic space
(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.06354pt{J}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.01353pt{J}\kern 2.625pt}}},B_{J})
, is given by the standard formula

(6.15.8)

s_{\alpha_{J}}(v)=v-\frac{2B_{J}(v,\alpha_{J})}{B_{J}(\alpha_{J},\alpha_{J})}\alpha_{J},\qquad v\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.06354pt{J}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.01353pt{J}\kern 2.625pt}}}.

There is a similar formula for the orthogonal reflection

s_{\alpha^{\prime}_{J^{\prime}}}
on
(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J^{\prime}}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-10.66483pt{J^{\prime}}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J^{\prime}}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-10.66483pt{J^{\prime}}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J^{\prime}}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-7.52603pt{J^{\prime}}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J^{\prime}}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-6.47603pt{J^{\prime}}\kern 2.625pt}}},B_{J^{\prime}})
in the vector
\alpha^{\prime}_{J^{\prime}}
.

Note that
\alpha_{J}=\pi_{M}(\alpha)
. There is a
B
-orthogonal direct sum decomposition

(6.15.9)

V=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{L}}}}V^{{\kern-6.05559pt\kern 6.44447pt}}_{{\kern-9.8903pt{L}\kern 6.44447pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{L}}}}V^{{\kern-6.05559pt\kern 6.44447pt}}_{{\kern-9.8903pt{L}\kern 6.44447pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{L}}}}V^{{\kern-3.55832pt\kern 3.9472pt}}_{{\kern-6.44026pt{L}\kern 3.9472pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{L}}}}V^{{\kern-2.43054pt\kern 2.81943pt}}_{{\kern-5.31248pt{L}\kern 2.81943pt}}}\oplus\operatorname{span}(\alpha_{J})\oplus\operatorname{span}(J\setminus M)\oplus\operatorname{span}(M)

in which the sum of the first two (resp., last two, first three, last three, second and fourth, first and third) factors is
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.06354pt{J}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.01353pt{J}\kern 2.625pt}}}
(resp.,
\operatorname{span}(J)
,
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{M}}}}V^{{\kern-6.05559pt\kern 6.44447pt}}_{{\kern-11.84349pt{M}\kern 6.44447pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{M}}}}V^{{\kern-6.05559pt\kern 6.44447pt}}_{{\kern-11.84349pt{M}\kern 6.44447pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{M}}}}V^{{\kern-3.55832pt\kern 3.9472pt}}_{{\kern-7.83539pt{M}\kern 3.9472pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{M}}}}V^{{\kern-2.43054pt\kern 2.81943pt}}_{{\kern-6.70761pt{M}\kern 2.81943pt}}}
,
\operatorname{span}(L)
,
\operatorname{span}(M\cup\{\alpha\})
,
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{M\cup\{\alpha\}}}}}V^{{\kern-6.05559pt\kern 6.44447pt}}_{{\kern-28.24472pt{M\cup\{\alpha\}}\kern 6.44447pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{M\cup\{\alpha\}}}}}V^{{\kern-6.05559pt\kern 6.44447pt}}_{{\kern-28.24472pt{M\cup\{\alpha\}}\kern 6.44447pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{M\cup\{\alpha\}}}}}V^{{\kern-3.55832pt\kern 3.9472pt}}_{{\kern-22.4077pt{M\cup\{\alpha\}}\kern 3.9472pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{M\cup\{\alpha\}}}}}V^{{\kern-2.43054pt\kern 2.81943pt}}_{{\kern-21.27992pt{M\cup\{\alpha\}}\kern 2.81943pt}}}
).

Recall that
\mathcal{V}(g)
is a linear map obtained by
restricting the domain and codomain of
w\colon V\to V

appropriately, where
w:=w_{M\cup\{\alpha\}}w_{M}
. It
follows that
\mathcal{V}(g)
acts as the identity on
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{M\cup\{\alpha\}}}}}V^{{\kern-6.05559pt\kern 6.44447pt}}_{{\kern-28.24472pt{M\cup\{\alpha\}}\kern 6.44447pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{M\cup\{\alpha\}}}}}V^{{\kern-6.05559pt\kern 6.44447pt}}_{{\kern-28.24472pt{M\cup\{\alpha\}}\kern 6.44447pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{M\cup\{\alpha\}}}}}V^{{\kern-3.55832pt\kern 3.9472pt}}_{{\kern-22.4077pt{M\cup\{\alpha\}}\kern 3.9472pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{M\cup\{\alpha\}}}}}V^{{\kern-2.43054pt\kern 2.81943pt}}_{{\kern-21.27992pt{M\cup\{\alpha\}}\kern 2.81943pt}}}
and maps
\alpha_{J}
to
-\alpha^{\prime}_{J^{\prime}}
. This determines the action of
w
on
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{M}}}}V^{{\kern-6.05559pt\kern 6.44447pt}}_{{\kern-11.84349pt{M}\kern 6.44447pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{M}}}}V^{{\kern-6.05559pt\kern 6.44447pt}}_{{\kern-11.84349pt{M}\kern 6.44447pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{M}}}}V^{{\kern-3.55832pt\kern 3.9472pt}}_{{\kern-7.83539pt{M}\kern 3.9472pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{M}}}}V^{{\kern-2.43054pt\kern 2.81943pt}}_{{\kern-6.70761pt{M}\kern 2.81943pt}}}{\,\supseteq\,}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.06354pt{J}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.01353pt{J}\kern 2.625pt}}}
. It is easily checked
that
w
and
\tau_{g}s_{\alpha_{J}}
both act in the same way on
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.06354pt{J}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.01353pt{J}\kern 2.625pt}}}
.
This proves the formula
\mathcal{V}(g)=\tau_{g}s_{\alpha_{J}}
. The corresponding formula for
g^{-1}
instead of
g
is
\mathcal{V}(g^{-1})=\tau_{g^{-1}}s^{\prime}_{\alpha^{\prime}_{J^{\prime}}}
. Taking inverses shows that

\mathcal{V}(g)=s^{\prime}_{\alpha^{\prime}_{J^{\prime}}}\tau_{g}
since
\tau_{g^{-1}}=(\tau_{g})^{-1}
.
Hence Proposition
1.63
(a) holds.

We may give an explicit formula for the linear map
\mathcal{V}(g)
as follows. For any
v\in V
, we have
v_{J}\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.06354pt{J}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.01353pt{J}\kern 2.625pt}}}
and (
6.15.4
) shows

v_{J}=v_{L}+\frac{B_{J}(v_{J},\alpha_{J})}{B_{J}(\alpha_{J},\alpha_{J})}\alpha_{J}=\left(v_{J}-\frac{B_{J}(v_{J},\alpha_{J})}{B_{J}(\alpha_{J},\alpha_{J})}\alpha_{J}\right)+\frac{B_{J}(v_{J},\alpha_{J})}{B_{J}(\alpha_{J},\alpha_{J})}\alpha_{J}.

Therefore,

(6.15.10)

gv_{J}:=(\mathcal{V}(g))(v_{J})=v_{J}-\frac{B_{J}(v_{J},\alpha_{J})}{B_{J}(\alpha_{J},\alpha_{J})}(\alpha_{J}+\alpha^{\prime}_{J^{\prime}})

If
v\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.06354pt{J}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{V}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.01353pt{J}\kern 2.625pt}}}
, then
v=v_{J}
and this simplifies to

(6.15.11)

(\mathcal{V}(g))(v)=v-\frac{B_{J}(v,\alpha_{J})}{B_{J}(\alpha_{J},\alpha_{J})}(\alpha_{J}+\alpha^{\prime}_{J^{\prime}}).

Also, for any
v\in V
,
(
6.15.5
) implies the following alternative form of
(
6.15.10
):

(6.15.12)

gv_{J}=v_{J^{\prime}}-\frac{B_{J}(v_{J},\alpha_{J})+B_{J^{\prime}}(v_{J^{\prime}},\alpha^{\prime}_{J^{\prime}})}{B_{J}(\alpha_{J},\alpha_{J})}\,\alpha^{\prime}_{J^{\prime}}.

It is straightforward to check that, if
\Pi
is linearly independent, then for
v=\beta
, where
\beta\in\Pi\setminus J
, this last formula reduces to
the assertion of Proposition
1.63
(b); in accordance with the case-wise definition of
\kappa_{g}(\beta_{J})
, one distinguishes the cases
\beta=\alpha
and
\beta\neq\alpha
in the argument.
∎

7.
Tits cones of realized Brink-Howlett groupoids

7.1.

We begin this section by discussing alternative descriptions of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{C}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{C}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{C}^{{\kern-3.15001pt\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-5.92743pt{J}\kern 3.5389pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{C}^{{\kern-2.13889pt\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-4.9163pt{J}\kern 2.52777pt}}}
and
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{X}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{X}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{X}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.06354pt{J}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\mathcal{X}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.01353pt{J}\kern 2.625pt}}}
, and their dependence on the realized root system used to define them.

Let
X
denote either
R
,
M
or
P
. Note that by Proposition
1.58
,

(7.1.1)

\begin{split}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt{X}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt{X}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-3.15001pt\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-5.92743pt{J}\kern 3.5389pt{X}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-2.13889pt\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-4.9163pt{J}\kern 2.52777pt{X}}}}:=&\{\,v\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.06354pt{J}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.01353pt{J}\kern 2.625pt}}}\mid B_{J}(v,{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}}^{+}_{X})\subseteq\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,\}\\
=&\{\,v\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.06354pt{J}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.01353pt{J}\kern 2.625pt}}}\mid B_{J}(v,\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-6.18059pt\kern 6.56947pt}}_{{\kern-9.86899pt{J}\kern 6.56947pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-6.18059pt\kern 6.56947pt}}_{{\kern-9.86899pt{J}\kern 6.56947pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-3.69446pt\kern 4.08334pt}}_{{\kern-6.47188pt{J}\kern 4.08334pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\Delta^{{\kern-2.52779pt\kern 2.91667pt}}_{{\kern-5.3052pt{J}\kern 2.91667pt}}})\subseteq\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,\}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-3.15001pt\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-5.92743pt{J}\kern 3.5389pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-2.13889pt\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-4.9163pt{J}\kern 2.52777pt}}}\end{split}

which is independent of
X
. Hence

(7.1.2)

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.06354pt{J}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.01353pt{J}\kern 2.625pt}}}_{X}:=\bigcup_{K\in\operatorname{ob}(G)}\,\bigcup_{w\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}G^{{\kern-5.04243pt\kern 5.3202pt}}_{{\kern-7.81985pt{J}\kern 5.3202pt{K}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}G^{{\kern-5.04243pt\kern 5.3202pt}}_{{\kern-7.81985pt{J}\kern 5.3202pt{K}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}G^{{\kern-2.47409pt\kern 2.75186pt}}_{{\kern-4.60081pt{J}\kern 2.75186pt{K}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}G^{{\kern-1.68785pt\kern 1.96562pt}}_{{\kern-3.81458pt{J}\kern 1.96562pt{K}}}}}({\mathcal{V}}(w))(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-10.33154pt{K}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-10.33154pt{K}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-3.15001pt\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-6.87291pt{K}\kern 3.5389pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-2.13889pt\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-5.86179pt{K}\kern 2.52777pt}}}_{X})=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.06354pt{J}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.01353pt{J}\kern 2.625pt}}}

is also independent of
X
.
(This is consistent with our convention of (possibly) omitting
X
from notation if
X=R
.)
Define

(7.1.3)

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-3.15001pt\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-5.92743pt{J}\kern 3.5389pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-2.13889pt\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-4.9163pt{J}\kern 2.52777pt}}}^{{\mathrm{re}}}_{X}:=\{\,v\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.06354pt{J}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{V}}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.01353pt{J}\kern 2.625pt}}}\mid B_{J}(v,{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}}^{{\mathrm{re}},+}_{X})\subseteq\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,\}

and

(7.1.4)

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.06354pt{J}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.01353pt{J}\kern 2.625pt}}}^{{\mathrm{re}}}_{X}=\bigcup_{K\in\operatorname{ob}(G)}\,\bigcup_{w\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}G^{{\kern-5.04243pt\kern 5.3202pt}}_{{\kern-7.81985pt{J}\kern 5.3202pt{K}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}G^{{\kern-5.04243pt\kern 5.3202pt}}_{{\kern-7.81985pt{J}\kern 5.3202pt{K}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}G^{{\kern-2.47409pt\kern 2.75186pt}}_{{\kern-4.60081pt{J}\kern 2.75186pt{K}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}G^{{\kern-1.68785pt\kern 1.96562pt}}_{{\kern-3.81458pt{J}\kern 1.96562pt{K}}}}}({\mathcal{V}}(w))(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-10.33154pt{K}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-10.33154pt{K}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-3.15001pt\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-6.87291pt{K}\kern 3.5389pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-2.13889pt\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-5.86179pt{K}\kern 2.52777pt}}}^{{\mathrm{re}}}_{X}),

and also make analogous definitions of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-3.15001pt\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-5.92743pt{J}\kern 3.5389pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-2.13889pt\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-4.9163pt{J}\kern 2.52777pt}}}^{{\mathrm{im}}}_{X}
and
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.06354pt{J}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.01353pt{J}\kern 2.625pt}}}^{{\mathrm{im}}}_{X}
by replacing “
{\mathrm{re}}
” with “
{\mathrm{im}}
” above. Then

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-3.15001pt\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-5.92743pt{J}\kern 3.5389pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-2.13889pt\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-4.9163pt{J}\kern 2.52777pt}}}^{{\mathrm{re}}}_{X}
is independent of
X
, by Theorem
1.33
(c) and Proposition
6.14
, and will be denoted as
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-3.15001pt\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-5.92743pt{J}\kern 3.5389pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-2.13889pt\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-4.9163pt{J}\kern 2.52777pt}}}^{{\mathrm{re}}}
. Hence

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.06354pt{J}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.01353pt{J}\kern 2.625pt}}}^{{\mathrm{re}}}_{X}
is also independent of
X
, and we may denote it as
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.06354pt{J}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.01353pt{J}\kern 2.625pt}}}^{{\mathrm{re}}}
.
Proposition
6.14
implies further that

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-3.15001pt\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-5.92743pt{J}\kern 3.5389pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-2.13889pt\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-4.9163pt{J}\kern 2.52777pt}}}^{{\mathrm{im}}}_{R}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-3.15001pt\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-5.92743pt{J}\kern 3.5389pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-2.13889pt\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-4.9163pt{J}\kern 2.52777pt}}}^{{\mathrm{im}}}_{M}
, so

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.06354pt{J}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.01353pt{J}\kern 2.625pt}}}^{{\mathrm{im}}}_{R}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-5.51393pt\kern 5.90282pt}}_{{\kern-9.20233pt{J}\kern 5.90282pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-3.28612pt\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-6.06354pt{J}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{{\kern-2.23611pt\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-5.01353pt{J}\kern 2.625pt}}}^{{\mathrm{im}}}_{M}
.
Since
{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}}^{+}_{X}={\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}}^{{\mathrm{re}},+}_{X}\cup{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}}^{{\mathrm{re}},+}_{X}
, we have

(7.1.5)

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-3.15001pt\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-5.92743pt{J}\kern 3.5389pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-2.13889pt\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-4.9163pt{J}\kern 2.52777pt}}}^{{\mathrm{re}}}_{X}\cap\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-3.15001pt\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-5.92743pt{J}\kern 3.5389pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-2.13889pt\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-4.9163pt{J}\kern 2.52777pt}}}^{{\mathrm{im}}}_{X}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-3.15001pt\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-5.92743pt{J}\kern 3.5389pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-2.13889pt\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-4.9163pt{J}\kern 2.52777pt}}}_{X}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-3.15001pt\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-5.92743pt{J}\kern 3.5389pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-2.13889pt\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-4.9163pt{J}\kern 2.52777pt}}},

independent of
X
.
For any morphism
(J,w,K)
in
G
, we have
{\mathcal{V}}(w)({\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-10.17876pt{K}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-10.17876pt{K}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.88959pt{K}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.88959pt{K}\kern 5.55557pt}}}}^{{\mathrm{im}},+}_{X})={\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-8.85509pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}\widetilde{\Upsilon}^{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-7.9441pt{J}\kern 5.55557pt}}}}^{{\mathrm{im}},+}_{X}
, by definition of imaginary roots, and hence

({\mathcal{V}}(w))(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-10.33154pt{K}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-10.33154pt{K}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-3.15001pt\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-6.87291pt{K}\kern 3.5389pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{K}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-2.13889pt\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-5.86179pt{K}\kern 2.52777pt}}}^{{\mathrm{im}}}_{X})\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-5.31947pt\kern 5.70836pt}}_{{\kern-9.00787pt{J}\kern 5.70836pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-3.15001pt\kern 3.5389pt}}_{{\kern-5.92743pt{J}\kern 3.5389pt}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{J}}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{{\kern-2.13889pt\kern 2.52777pt}}_{{\kern-4.9163pt{J}\kern 2.52777pt}}}^{{\mathrm{im}}}_{X}
. By definition of
